Yeah… I mean, yes, that’s kind of what it feels like. Like other people might just hear a normal comment in class or see a Slack message and move on, but to me it feels like there’s another layer to it.

Not really through, like, media or random objects… I mean, sometimes I’ll overthink a headline or something someone says at a coffee shop, but the thing I came in about is much more specific.

Mostly I think they’re meant to help me. That’s the part that makes it feel hard to just ignore, because it feels like… like they’re coordinating opportunities, not trying to hurt me.

I came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly setting things up or pointing me toward something. Like a comment in class, or something in Slack, will feel coded—like it’s not just about the assignment, it’s actually about where my career is supposed to go.

I don’t really think they’re trying to mislead me. I mean, sometimes I get nervous that I’m missing the signal or interpreting it wrong, and then I’ll spend way too long thinking about it. But it feels more like guidance than harm. Like when we did this whole operations case about bottlenecks and hidden constraints, I kept thinking, that’s exactly what this is—there’s some system underneath and I’m supposed to notice it. Sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked. But no, I don’t think it’s to hurt me. Mostly to help or steer me.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… I came in because I know this is going to sound kind of hard to explain, but I’m really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor I know are sort of quietly coordinating opportunities for me.\n\nLike, sometimes in class someone will make a comment, or there’ll be a Slack message, and it feels like it’s not just random—it’s coded, like a signal about where my career is supposed to go. And I know that sounds strange when I say it out loud, but it’s been hard to ignore. I mean, I work in operations, so I’m usually pretty good at picking up patterns, and in business school everyone is always networking anyway, so it’s not totally out of nowhere… but this feels more directed at me.\n\nDoctor: When you say they’re sending you signals about your career, do you feel like they’re trying to give you special or secret messages that others wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, that’s kind of what it feels like. Like other people might just hear a normal comment in class or see a Slack message and move on, but to me it feels like there’s another layer to it.\n\nIt’s not like they’re saying, “Hey, this is a secret message,” obviously, because that would be too direct. It’s more like… certain phrases, timing, who reacts to what, and then the startup advisor will say something later that seems to match it. And then I start thinking, okay, they’re coordinating something, maybe pointing me toward the right opportunity or company or path.\n\nSorry, I know I’m rambling a little. Business school is already so much about reading between the lines and networking, so it’s hard to explain why this feels different, but it really does feel directed at me in a way other people wouldn’t necessarily pick up on.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages are part of a bigger plan that involves guiding or preparing you for something important?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s what makes it feel so intense. It doesn’t feel like just random networking or normal MBA stuff. It feels like there’s a bigger plan, like they’re trying to guide me toward the right career move or prepare me for some opportunity I’m supposed to notice.\n\nAnd I know in business school everyone is always talking about “positioning” and “strategy,” like even our operations cases are all about seeing the system behind the surface problem, so maybe that’s why I pick up on it. But with this classmate and the startup advisor, it feels different—like the comments in class or Slack messages are coded signals, and I’m supposed to connect them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these signals are part of a more general effort to guide or influence you, not just in your career but in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe, but I notice it mostly around my career. That’s where it feels the strongest. Like, I came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced that this one MBA classmate and the startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me.\n\nIt’s like certain comments in class or Slack messages aren’t just normal comments, they’re coded signals about where I’m supposed to go professionally. And maybe that spills into other things a little, because business school sort of takes over your whole life—your friends, your schedule, even what you talk about at dinner—but I wouldn’t say I have a clean answer beyond that. The main thing is the career path, the opportunities, like they’re trying to point me toward something.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are sending you secret messages or trying to communicate with you in unusual ways outside of your MBA class or career context, like through media, objects, or unrelated people?\n\nPatient: Not really through, like, media or random objects… I mean, sometimes I’ll overthink a headline or something someone says at a coffee shop, but the thing I came in about is much more specific.\n\nIt’s really this one classmate in my MBA program and this startup advisor. I’ve been pretty convinced they’re coordinating in this quiet way, like comments in class or Slack messages have this coded meaning about where my career is supposed to go. And I know that sounds strange when I say it out loud.\n\nIt’s just—business school already makes everything feel connected, like networking events, group projects, who gets invited where… sorry, I’m drifting. But no, it’s mostly around them and the career stuff, not like the TV is talking to me or objects are sending messages.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the messages or signals you’re picking up are meant to help you, or do you ever worry they might be meant to mislead or harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: Mostly I think they’re meant to help me. That’s the part that makes it feel hard to just ignore, because it feels like… like they’re coordinating opportunities, not trying to hurt me.\n\nI came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly setting things up or pointing me toward something. Like a comment in class, or something in Slack, will feel coded—like it’s not just about the assignment, it’s actually about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI don’t really think they’re trying to mislead me. I mean, sometimes I get nervous that I’m missing the signal or interpreting it wrong, and then I’ll spend way too long thinking about it. But it feels more like guidance than harm. Like when we did this whole operations case about bottlenecks and hidden constraints, I kept thinking, that’s exactly what this is—there’s some system underneath and I’m supposed to notice it. Sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked. But no, I don’t think it’s to hurt me. I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission,” exactly… that sounds a little dramatic when I hear it said that way. But it does feel like there’s something I’m supposed to understand that other people aren’t seeing.

Like I came in because I’ve been really convinced that this one classmate from my MBA program and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Not openly, but through certain comments in class or Slack messages, like there’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.

And business school is already so full of those little hints, you know? Like who gets pulled into what project, who mentions a founder casually, who reacts to something in a group chat… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess, yes, it feels kind of specific to me, like I’m the one meant to pick up on it and act on it. But I don’t think of myself as, like, chosen in some huge way. It’s more like there’s a path being pointed out and I’m supposed to notice it.

And I know that sounds strange when I say it out loud, but it’s been hard to ignore. I mean, I work in operations, so I’m usually pretty good at picking up patterns, and in business school everyone is always networking anyway, so it’s not totally out of nowhere… but this feels more directed at me.\n\nDoctor: When you say they’re sending you signals about your career, do you feel like they’re trying to give you special or secret messages that others wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, that’s kind of what it feels like. Like other people might just hear a normal comment in class or see a Slack message and move on, but to me it feels like there’s another layer to it.\n\nIt’s not like they’re saying, “Hey, this is a secret message,” obviously, because that would be too direct. It’s more like… certain phrases, timing, who reacts to what, and then the startup advisor will say something later that seems to match it. And then I start thinking, okay, they’re coordinating something, maybe pointing me toward the right opportunity or company or path.\n\nSorry, I know I’m rambling a little. Business school is already so much about reading between the lines and networking, so it’s hard to explain why this feels different, but it really does feel directed at me in a way other people wouldn’t necessarily pick up on.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages are part of a bigger plan that involves guiding or preparing you for something important?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s what makes it feel so intense. It doesn’t feel like just random networking or normal MBA stuff. It feels like there’s a bigger plan, like they’re trying to guide me toward the right career move or prepare me for some opportunity I’m supposed to notice.\n\nAnd I know in business school everyone is always talking about “positioning” and “strategy,” like even our operations cases are all about seeing the system behind the surface problem, so maybe that’s why I pick up on it. But with this classmate and the startup advisor, it feels different—like the comments in class or Slack messages are coded signals, and I’m supposed to connect them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these signals are part of a more general effort to guide or influence you, not just in your career but in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe, but I notice it mostly around my career. That’s where it feels the strongest. Like, I came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced that this one MBA classmate and the startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me.\n\nIt’s like certain comments in class or Slack messages aren’t just normal comments, they’re coded signals about where I’m supposed to go professionally. And maybe that spills into other things a little, because business school sort of takes over your whole life—your friends, your schedule, even what you talk about at dinner—but I wouldn’t say I have a clean answer beyond that. The main thing is the career path, the opportunities, like they’re trying to point me toward something.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are sending you secret messages or trying to communicate with you in unusual ways outside of your MBA class or career context, like through media, objects, or unrelated people?\n\nPatient: Not really through, like, media or random objects… I mean, sometimes I’ll overthink a headline or something someone says at a coffee shop, but the thing I came in about is much more specific.\n\nIt’s really this one classmate in my MBA program and this startup advisor. I’ve been pretty convinced they’re coordinating in this quiet way, like comments in class or Slack messages have this coded meaning about where my career is supposed to go. And I know that sounds strange when I say it out loud.\n\nIt’s just—business school already makes everything feel connected, like networking events, group projects, who gets invited where… sorry, I’m drifting. But no, it’s mostly around them and the career stuff, not like the TV is talking to me or objects are sending messages.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the messages or signals you’re picking up are meant to help you, or do you ever worry they might be meant to mislead or harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: Mostly I think they’re meant to help me. That’s the part that makes it feel hard to just ignore, because it feels like… like they’re coordinating opportunities, not trying to hurt me.\n\nI came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly setting things up or pointing me toward something. Like a comment in class, or something in Slack, will feel coded—like it’s not just about the assignment, it’s actually about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI don’t really think they’re trying to mislead me. I mean, sometimes I get nervous that I’m missing the signal or interpreting it wrong, and then I’ll spend way too long thinking about it. But it feels more like guidance than harm. Like when we did this whole operations case about bottlenecks and hidden constraints, I kept thinking, that’s exactly what this is—there’s some system underneath and I’m supposed to notice it. Sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked. But no, I don’t think it’s to hurt me. Mostly to help or steer me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the signals or messages you’re receiving are part of a special role or mission that only you can understand or carry out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission,” exactly… that sounds a little dramatic when I hear it said that way. But it does feel like there’s something I’m supposed to understand that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nLike I came in because I’ve been really convinced that this one classmate from my MBA program and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Not openly, but through certain comments in class or Slack messages, like there’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd business school is already so full of those little hints, you know? Like who gets pulled into what project, who mentions a founder casually, who reacts to something in a group chat… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess, yes, it feels kind of specific to me, like I’m the one meant to pick up on it and act on it. But I don’t think of myself as, like, chosen in some huge way. It’s more like there’s a path being pointed out and I’m supposed to notice it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Outside of that… not really? I mean, people have told me that this specific thing doesn’t make sense, or that I’m “reading into it,” which I get, sort of.

But I came in because I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel coded, like they’re signals about where my career is supposed to go.

And I know MBA programs are full of networking and subtle stuff anyway—like someone mentions a company and suddenly three people are applying there, or a professor says something that changes how you think about your whole path—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. But no, outside of that classmate and advisor thing, I can’t really think of another belief like that.

Sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked. But no, I don’t think it’s to hurt me. Mostly to help or steer me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the signals or messages you’re receiving are part of a special role or mission that only you can understand or carry out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission,” exactly… that sounds a little dramatic when I hear it said that way. But it does feel like there’s something I’m supposed to understand that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nLike I came in because I’ve been really convinced that this one classmate from my MBA program and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Not openly, but through certain comments in class or Slack messages, like there’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd business school is already so full of those little hints, you know? Like who gets pulled into what project, who mentions a founder casually, who reacts to something in a group chat… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess, yes, it feels kind of specific to me, like I’m the one meant to pick up on it and act on it. But I don’t think of myself as, like, chosen in some huge way. It’s more like there’s a path being pointed out and I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that others have told you are not true or don’t make sense, outside of what you’ve been describing about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Outside of that… not really? I mean, people have told me that this specific thing doesn’t make sense, or that I’m “reading into it,” which I get, sort of.\n\nBut I came in because I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel coded, like they’re signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know MBA programs are full of networking and subtle stuff anyway—like someone mentions a company and suddenly three people are applying there, or a professor says something that changes how you think about your whole path—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. But no, outside of that classmate and advisor thing, I can’t really think of another belief like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know most people would find strange or impossible, like being able to read minds, control events with your thoughts, or being part of a secret mission that no one else knows about?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 25 / general_or_operations_manager / bachelors\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like certain comments in class or Slack messages are coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think I can read minds or control things with my thoughts, no. That sounds… like, I wouldn’t say that.

The closest thing is what I’ve been trying to explain, where I came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class, or Slack messages, feel like they’re coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.

And I know that probably sounds strange to most people. Even saying it out loud, I can hear how it sounds. But in the moment it feels connected, like the pieces line up. Business school is already so much about reading between the lines—who knows who, what someone really means when they say they’ll “make an intro,” all of that—so it doesn’t feel impossible to me exactly. It just feels like there’s another layer I’m supposed to notice.

It just feels like there’s another layer I’m supposed to notice.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had experiences or beliefs where you felt certain something was true, even when there was no evidence or others strongly disagreed, outside of what you’ve described about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Not really outside of that, no. I mean… I know I keep circling back to the same thing, but that’s the thing that’s been so stuck in my head.

Like other people might just hear a normal comment in class or see a Slack message and move on, but to me it feels like there’s another layer to it.\n\nIt’s not like they’re saying, “Hey, this is a secret message,” obviously, because that would be too direct. It’s more like… certain phrases, timing, who reacts to what, and then the startup advisor will say something later that seems to match it. And then I start thinking, okay, they’re coordinating something, maybe pointing me toward the right opportunity or company or path.\n\nSorry, I know I’m rambling a little. Business school is already so much about reading between the lines and networking, so it’s hard to explain why this feels different, but it really does feel directed at me in a way other people wouldn’t necessarily pick up on.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages are part of a bigger plan that involves guiding or preparing you for something important?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s what makes it feel so intense. It doesn’t feel like just random networking or normal MBA stuff. It feels like there’s a bigger plan, like they’re trying to guide me toward the right career move or prepare me for some opportunity I’m supposed to notice.\n\nAnd I know in business school everyone is always talking about “positioning” and “strategy,” like even our operations cases are all about seeing the system behind the surface problem, so maybe that’s why I pick up on it. But with this classmate and the startup advisor, it feels different—like the comments in class or Slack messages are coded signals, and I’m supposed to connect them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these signals are part of a more general effort to guide or influence you, not just in your career but in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe, but I notice it mostly around my career. That’s where it feels the strongest. Like, I came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced that this one MBA classmate and the startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me.\n\nIt’s like certain comments in class or Slack messages aren’t just normal comments, they’re coded signals about where I’m supposed to go professionally. And maybe that spills into other things a little, because business school sort of takes over your whole life—your friends, your schedule, even what you talk about at dinner—but I wouldn’t say I have a clean answer beyond that. The main thing is the career path, the opportunities, like they’re trying to point me toward something.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are sending you secret messages or trying to communicate with you in unusual ways outside of your MBA class or career context, like through media, objects, or unrelated people?\n\nPatient: Not really through, like, media or random objects… I mean, sometimes I’ll overthink a headline or something someone says at a coffee shop, but the thing I came in about is much more specific.\n\nIt’s really this one classmate in my MBA program and this startup advisor. I’ve been pretty convinced they’re coordinating in this quiet way, like comments in class or Slack messages have this coded meaning about where my career is supposed to go. And I know that sounds strange when I say it out loud.\n\nIt’s just—business school already makes everything feel connected, like networking events, group projects, who gets invited where… sorry, I’m drifting. But no, it’s mostly around them and the career stuff, not like the TV is talking to me or objects are sending messages.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the messages or signals you’re picking up are meant to help you, or do you ever worry they might be meant to mislead or harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: Mostly I think they’re meant to help me. That’s the part that makes it feel hard to just ignore, because it feels like… like they’re coordinating opportunities, not trying to hurt me.\n\nI came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly setting things up or pointing me toward something. Like a comment in class, or something in Slack, will feel coded—like it’s not just about the assignment, it’s actually about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI don’t really think they’re trying to mislead me. I mean, sometimes I get nervous that I’m missing the signal or interpreting it wrong, and then I’ll spend way too long thinking about it. But it feels more like guidance than harm. Like when we did this whole operations case about bottlenecks and hidden constraints, I kept thinking, that’s exactly what this is—there’s some system underneath and I’m supposed to notice it. Sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked. But no, I don’t think it’s to hurt me. Mostly to help or steer me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the signals or messages you’re receiving are part of a special role or mission that only you can understand or carry out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission,” exactly… that sounds a little dramatic when I hear it said that way. But it does feel like there’s something I’m supposed to understand that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nLike I came in because I’ve been really convinced that this one classmate from my MBA program and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Not openly, but through certain comments in class or Slack messages, like there’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd business school is already so full of those little hints, you know? Like who gets pulled into what project, who mentions a founder casually, who reacts to something in a group chat… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess, yes, it feels kind of specific to me, like I’m the one meant to pick up on it and act on it. But I don’t think of myself as, like, chosen in some huge way. It’s more like there’s a path being pointed out and I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that others have told you are not true or don’t make sense, outside of what you’ve been describing about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Outside of that… not really? I mean, people have told me that this specific thing doesn’t make sense, or that I’m “reading into it,” which I get, sort of.\n\nBut I came in because I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel coded, like they’re signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know MBA programs are full of networking and subtle stuff anyway—like someone mentions a company and suddenly three people are applying there, or a professor says something that changes how you think about your whole path—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. But no, outside of that classmate and advisor thing, I can’t really think of another belief like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know most people would find strange or impossible, like being able to read minds, control events with your thoughts, or being part of a secret mission that no one else knows about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can read minds or control things with my thoughts, no. That sounds… like, I wouldn’t say that.\n\nThe closest thing is what I’ve been trying to explain, where I came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class, or Slack messages, feel like they’re coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds strange to most people. Even saying it out loud, I can hear how it sounds. But in the moment it feels connected, like the pieces line up. Business school is already so much about reading between the lines—who knows who, what someone really means when they say they’ll “make an intro,” all of that—so it doesn’t feel impossible to me exactly. It just feels like there’s another layer I’m supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences or beliefs where you felt certain something was true, even when there was no evidence or others strongly disagreed, outside of what you’ve described about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. I guess… I keep thinking of that same example because it’s the only one that feels big enough to count. Like when we did this whole operations case about bottlenecks and hidden constraints, I kept thinking, that’s exactly what this is—there’s some system underneath and I’m supposed to notice it. Sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked. But no, I don’t think it’s to hurt me. Mostly to help or steer me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the signals or messages you’re receiving are part of a special role or mission that only you can understand or carry out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission,” exactly… that sounds a little dramatic when I hear it said that way. But it does feel like there’s something I’m supposed to understand that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nLike I came in because I’ve been really convinced that this one classmate from my MBA program and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like who gets pulled into what project, who mentions a founder casually, who reacts to something in a group chat… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess, yes, it feels kind of specific to me, like I’m the one meant to pick up on it and act on it. But I don’t think of myself as, like, chosen in some huge way. It’s more like there’s a path being pointed out and I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that others have told you are not true or don’t make sense, outside of what you’ve been describing about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Outside of that… not really? But no, outside of that classmate and advisor thing, I can’t really think of another belief like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know most people would find strange or impossible, like being able to read minds, control events with your thoughts, or being part of a secret mission that no one else knows about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can read minds or control things with my thoughts, no. Yeah… I think that’s kind of the part that scares me, because there are moments where I can step back and say, okay, this probably isn’t literally true. Like, I know it sounds hard to explain.

Sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked. But no, I don’t think it’s to hurt me. Mostly to help or steer me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the signals or messages you’re receiving are part of a special role or mission that only you can understand or carry out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission,” exactly… that sounds a little dramatic when I hear it said that way. But it does feel like there’s something I’m supposed to understand that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nLike I came in because I’ve been really convinced that this one classmate from my MBA program and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like who gets pulled into what project, who mentions a founder casually, who reacts to something in a group chat… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess, yes, it feels kind of specific to me, like I’m the one meant to pick up on it and act on it. But I don’t think of myself as, like, chosen in some huge way. It’s more like there’s a path being pointed out and I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that others have told you are not true or don’t make sense, outside of what you’ve been describing about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Outside of that… not really? Like certain comments in class, or Slack messages, feel like they’re coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds strange to most people. It just feels like there’s another layer I’m supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences or beliefs where you felt certain something was true, even when there was no evidence or others strongly disagreed, outside of what you’ve described about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. I know you keep asking “not related to them,” and I’m trying to think of something else, but honestly that’s the main thing that feels… unrealistic when I say it out loud.

I came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like, I came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced that this one MBA classmate and the startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me.\n\nIt’s like certain comments in class or Slack messages aren’t just normal comments, they’re coded signals about where I’m supposed to go professionally. And maybe that spills into other things a little, because business school sort of takes over your whole life—your friends, your schedule, even what you talk about at dinner—but I wouldn’t say I have a clean answer beyond that. The main thing is the career path, the opportunities, like they’re trying to point me toward something.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are sending you secret messages or trying to communicate with you in unusual ways outside of your MBA class or career context, like through media, objects, or unrelated people?\n\nPatient: Not really through, like, media or random objects… I mean, sometimes I’ll overthink a headline or something someone says at a coffee shop, but the thing I came in about is much more specific.\n\nIt’s really this one classmate in my MBA program and this startup advisor. I’ve been pretty convinced they’re coordinating in this quiet way, like comments in class or Slack messages have this coded meaning about where my career is supposed to go. And I know that sounds strange when I say it out loud.\n\nIt’s just—business school already makes everything feel connected, like networking events, group projects, who gets invited where… sorry, I’m drifting. But no, it’s mostly around them and the career stuff, not like the TV is talking to me or objects are sending messages.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the messages or signals you’re picking up are meant to help you, or do you ever worry they might be meant to mislead or harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: Mostly I think they’re meant to help me. That’s the part that makes it feel hard to just ignore, because it feels like… like they’re coordinating opportunities, not trying to hurt me.\n\nI came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly setting things up or pointing me toward something. Like a comment in class, or something in Slack, will feel coded—like it’s not just about the assignment, it’s actually about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI don’t really think they’re trying to mislead me. I mean, sometimes I get nervous that I’m missing the signal or interpreting it wrong, and then I’ll spend way too long thinking about it. But it feels more like guidance than harm. Like when we did this whole operations case about bottlenecks and hidden constraints, I kept thinking, that’s exactly what this is—there’s some system underneath and I’m supposed to notice it. Sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked. But no, I don’t think it’s to hurt me. Mostly to help or steer me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the signals or messages you’re receiving are part of a special role or mission that only you can understand or carry out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission,” exactly… that sounds a little dramatic when I hear it said that way. But it does feel like there’s something I’m supposed to understand that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nLike I came in because I’ve been really convinced that this one classmate from my MBA program and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Not openly, but through certain comments in class or Slack messages, like there’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd business school is already so full of those little hints, you know? Like who gets pulled into what project, who mentions a founder casually, who reacts to something in a group chat… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess, yes, it feels kind of specific to me, like I’m the one meant to pick up on it and act on it. But I don’t think of myself as, like, chosen in some huge way. It’s more like there’s a path being pointed out and I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that others have told you are not true or don’t make sense, outside of what you’ve been describing about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Outside of that… not really? I mean, people have told me that this specific thing doesn’t make sense, or that I’m “reading into it,” which I get, sort of.\n\nBut I came in because I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel coded, like they’re signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know MBA programs are full of networking and subtle stuff anyway—like someone mentions a company and suddenly three people are applying there, or a professor says something that changes how you think about your whole path—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. But no, outside of that classmate and advisor thing, I can’t really think of another belief like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know most people would find strange or impossible, like being able to read minds, control events with your thoughts, or being part of a secret mission that no one else knows about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can read minds or control things with my thoughts, no. That sounds… like, I wouldn’t say that.\n\nThe closest thing is what I’ve been trying to explain, where I came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class, or Slack messages, feel like they’re coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds strange to most people. Even saying it out loud, I can hear how it sounds. But in the moment it feels connected, like the pieces line up. Business school is already so much about reading between the lines—who knows who, what someone really means when they say they’ll “make an intro,” all of that—so it doesn’t feel impossible to me exactly. It just feels like there’s another layer I’m supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences or beliefs where you felt certain something was true, even when there was no evidence or others strongly disagreed, outside of what you’ve described about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. I mean… I know I keep circling back to the same thing, but that’s the thing that’s been so stuck in my head.\n\nI came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class, or Slack messages, feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd people have told me that’s not evidence, or that I’m connecting things that aren’t connected. I understand why they say that. But when it happens, it feels really clear to me, like I’m supposed to notice it. Outside of that specific situation, though, I can’t think of another belief like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any strong beliefs or ideas that you felt completely certain about, even when others told you they weren’t true or didn’t make sense, outside of what you’ve described about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Other than that specific thing, I don’t think so. I know I keep coming back to it, but that’s really the one that feels… fixed, I guess.\n\nI came in because I’ve been completely convinced that this MBA classmate of mine and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like a comment in class, or something someone drops in Slack, can feel like it’s not just random—it’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know people tell me that doesn’t make sense, or that it’s just normal business school networking and I’m overinterpreting it. Which, MBA people do talk in this weird indirect way all the time—like everyone is always hinting at referrals and opportunities—so it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal from what feels directed at me. But outside of that, no, I can’t think of another belief like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or thoughts that you know most people would say are impossible or make no sense at all, even if they aren’t about your MBA classmate or the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: I mean… not like impossible-impossible, like I don’t think I can bend physics or anything. It’s more this same thing I keep getting stuck on, and I know you said even if it isn’t about them, but that’s honestly the only one that comes up for me.\n\nI came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like, someone says something in class, or there’s a Slack message that looks normal to everyone else, and I’ll feel like it’s actually a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know most people would probably say that makes no sense. My friends would say, “That’s just networking,” or “You’re reading too much into it.” But business school is so weird with all the hidden meanings and who’s connected to who, so it doesn’t feel completely impossible to me in the moment. It feels like I’m supposed to notice it. Outside of that… I don’t really have another example.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or ideas that you know most people would say are impossible or make no sense at all, even if they aren’t about your MBA classmate or the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: I guess… I keep thinking of that same example because it’s the only one that feels big enough to count. I know most people would probably say it doesn’t make sense, but I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me.\n\nLike, someone will make a comment in class, or I’ll see a Slack message, and it feels like it’s not just normal chatter—it’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go. And then I start thinking about it a lot, like maybe I’m supposed to follow that clue.\n\nIt’s hard because business school already feels like everyone is hinting at things all the time, like internships and connections and who knows who, so I can get kind of pulled into that. But outside of that specific thing… no, I don’t think I have other beliefs that are impossible like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts or beliefs that you know are definitely not true, but you still can’t help believing them, even for a moment?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s kind of the part that scares me, because there are moments where I can step back and say, okay, this probably isn’t literally true. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really TV or music, I don’t think. I mean, sometimes I’ll overhear people talking and my mind kind of jumps, like, “Was that about me?” but the main thing is still school stuff.

I’ve been pretty convinced they’re coordinating in this quiet way, like comments in class or Slack messages have this coded meaning about where my career is supposed to go. And I know that sounds strange when I say it out loud.\n\nIt’s just—business school already makes everything feel connected, like networking events, group projects, who gets invited where… sorry, I’m drifting. But no, it’s mostly around them and the career stuff, not like the TV is talking to me or objects are sending messages.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the messages or signals you’re picking up are meant to help you, or do you ever worry they might be meant to mislead or harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: Mostly I think they’re meant to help me. That’s the part that makes it feel hard to just ignore, because it feels like… like they’re coordinating opportunities, not trying to hurt me.\n\nI came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been really convinced one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly setting things up or pointing me toward something. Like a comment in class, or something in Slack, will feel coded—like it’s not just about the assignment, it’s actually about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI don’t really think they’re trying to mislead me. I mean, sometimes I get nervous that I’m missing the signal or interpreting it wrong, and then I’ll spend way too long thinking about it. But it feels more like guidance than harm. Like when we did this whole operations case about bottlenecks and hidden constraints, I kept thinking, that’s exactly what this is—there’s some system underneath and I’m supposed to notice it. Sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked. But no, I don’t think it’s to hurt me. Mostly to help or steer me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the signals or messages you’re receiving are part of a special role or mission that only you can understand or carry out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission,” exactly… that sounds a little dramatic when I hear it said that way. But it does feel like there’s something I’m supposed to understand that other people aren’t seeing.\n\nLike I came in because I’ve been really convinced that this one classmate from my MBA program and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Not openly, but through certain comments in class or Slack messages, like there’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd business school is already so full of those little hints, you know? Like who gets pulled into what project, who mentions a founder casually, who reacts to something in a group chat… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess, yes, it feels kind of specific to me, like I’m the one meant to pick up on it and act on it. But I don’t think of myself as, like, chosen in some huge way. It’s more like there’s a path being pointed out and I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that others have told you are not true or don’t make sense, outside of what you’ve been describing about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Outside of that… not really? I mean, people have told me that this specific thing doesn’t make sense, or that I’m “reading into it,” which I get, sort of.\n\nBut I came in because I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel coded, like they’re signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know MBA programs are full of networking and subtle stuff anyway—like someone mentions a company and suddenly three people are applying there, or a professor says something that changes how you think about your whole path—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. But no, outside of that classmate and advisor thing, I can’t really think of another belief like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know most people would find strange or impossible, like being able to read minds, control events with your thoughts, or being part of a secret mission that no one else knows about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can read minds or control things with my thoughts, no. That sounds… like, I wouldn’t say that.\n\nThe closest thing is what I’ve been trying to explain, where I came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class, or Slack messages, feel like they’re coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds strange to most people. Even saying it out loud, I can hear how it sounds. But in the moment it feels connected, like the pieces line up. Business school is already so much about reading between the lines—who knows who, what someone really means when they say they’ll “make an intro,” all of that—so it doesn’t feel impossible to me exactly. It just feels like there’s another layer I’m supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences or beliefs where you felt certain something was true, even when there was no evidence or others strongly disagreed, outside of what you’ve described about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. I mean… I know I keep circling back to the same thing, but that’s the thing that’s been so stuck in my head.\n\nI came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class, or Slack messages, feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd people have told me that’s not evidence, or that I’m connecting things that aren’t connected. I understand why they say that. But when it happens, it feels really clear to me, like I’m supposed to notice it. Outside of that specific situation, though, I can’t think of another belief like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any strong beliefs or ideas that you felt completely certain about, even when others told you they weren’t true or didn’t make sense, outside of what you’ve described about your MBA classmate and the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: Other than that specific thing, I don’t think so. I know I keep coming back to it, but that’s really the one that feels… fixed, I guess.\n\nI came in because I’ve been completely convinced that this MBA classmate of mine and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like a comment in class, or something someone drops in Slack, can feel like it’s not just random—it’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd I know people tell me that doesn’t make sense, or that it’s just normal business school networking and I’m overinterpreting it. It’s more this same thing I keep getting stuck on, and I know you said even if it isn’t about them, but that’s honestly the only one that comes up for me.\n\nI came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Outside of that… I don’t really have another example.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or ideas that you know most people would say are impossible or make no sense at all, even if they aren’t about your MBA classmate or the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: I guess… I keep thinking of that same example because it’s the only one that feels big enough to count. I know most people would probably say it doesn’t make sense, but I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me.\n\nLike, someone will make a comment in class, or I’ll see a Slack message, and it feels like it’s not just normal chatter—it’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go. And then I start thinking about it a lot, like maybe I’m supposed to follow that clue.\n\nIt’s hard because business school already feels like everyone is hinting at things all the time, like internships and connections and who knows who, so I can get kind of pulled into that. But outside of that specific thing… no, I don’t think I have other beliefs that are impossible like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts or beliefs that you know are definitely not true, but you still can’t help believing them, even for a moment?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s kind of the part that scares me, because there are moments where I can step back and say, okay, this probably isn’t literally true. Like, I know it sounds hard to explain.\n\nBut then I still get pulled back into it. I’ll see a Slack message or hear something in class, and suddenly I’m completely convinced my MBA classmate and this startup advisor are sort of coordinating opportunities for me, like it’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd then I’ll start connecting it to other stuff, like who posted what or who mentioned a company, and I know business school is already full of people being vague and strategic, so it’s not like… I don’t know, it doesn’t feel totally random in the moment. But yes, there are times I know I’m probably reading too much into it, and I still can’t stop believing it for a bit.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or ideas that you know are completely unrealistic or impossible, even if they’re not related to your MBA classmate or the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: I know you keep asking “not related to them,” and I’m trying to think of something else, but honestly that’s the main thing that feels… unrealistic when I say it out loud.\n\nI came in because I know this sounds hard to explain, but I’ve been completely convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class, or even Slack messages that everyone else probably reads as totally normal, feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd then I’ll start thinking, okay, was that comment meant for me? Was I supposed to notice that company name? It spirals a little. Outside of that, I’m not really thinking I can do impossible things or anything like that. It’s more this one belief that keeps pulling me in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like someone was trying to harm you, control your thoughts, or that thoughts were being put into your mind or taken out?\n\nPatient: Not really like harming me or controlling my thoughts, no. It’s more… I don’t know, it’s hard to separate because business school already feels so full of little signals and people networking in these weird indirect ways.\n\nThe thing I came in about is that I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like, someone says something in class or there’s a Slack message, and it feels coded, like it’s pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go.\n\nBut I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. It’s more that I feel like I’m supposed to notice what they’re hinting at.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or experiences that you know most people would say are completely impossible, like thoughts being put into your mind by outside forces, or being able to predict or control events in ways that can’t be explained?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like thoughts being put in my mind or me controlling events. That sounds… more extreme than what I mean.\n\nThe thing I keep coming back to is what I said before, that I know it sounds strange, but I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and this startup advisor are sort of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like someone will make a comment in class, or there’ll be a Slack message, and it feels like it’s not just a normal message, it’s a coded signal about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nAnd then I’ll start connecting it to other things, like who mentioned which company or who reacted to something, and I know most people would probably say, “That’s not what’s happening.” But in the moment it feels really real.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things in your environment, like TV shows, music, or strangers’ conversations, were sending you personal messages meant only for you, even if they weren’t directed at you?\n\nPatient: Not really TV or music, I don’t think. I mean, sometimes I’ll overhear people talking and my mind kind of jumps, like, “Was that about me?” but the main thing is still school stuff.\n\nLike, I came in because it got hard to ignore that I’ve been completely convinced this MBA classmate and a startup advisor are sort of coordinating things for me. And I know that sounds strange. But certain comments in class or Slack messages feel like coded signals, like they’re pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go.\n\nIt’s not like a song on the radio is talking to me, exactly. It’s more the class and startup environment, because everyone is always hinting and networking and saying things indirectly anyway, so it starts to feel like there’s a message in it for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that you later found out was completely untrue, even if it wasn’t related to your MBA classmate or the startup advisor?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one that’s totally separate, but I keep circling back to the same thing, honestly. I mean, there are normal things where I’ve misread someone’s tone or thought someone was mad at me and they weren’t, but that’s not really the same.\n\nThe thing that feels strongest is still why I came in — being really convinced that my MBA classmate and this startup advisor were quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like a comment in class, or something on Slack, would feel like a coded signal about where my career was supposed to go.\n\nAnd then I’d start noticing little patterns, like who responded to what, or why someone mentioned a certain company. Sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked. I’m not sure I have a separate example outside of that."
